## What is the problem
Getting into touch typing can be frustrating because the cognitive load is too high. You have to map 40+ keys to 8 fingers. It's easy to remember *which hand* to use for a key, but *which finger* to use in that hand? that's much harder.

Instead of mapping each key to the hand that types it and then *directly* to the specific finger (by its position within that hand).

transform="translate(29.29146411770165 155.2314832730054) rotate(0 24.03997802734375 12.5)"><text x="0" y="19.08" font-family="Nunito, sans-serif, Segoe UI Emoji" font-size="20px" fill="#000000" text-anchor="start" style="white-space: pre;" direction="ltr" dominant-baseline="alphabetic">pinky</text></g></svg>

And then memorize every key, not just as the character, but as a character and a color (that indicates which finger types it).

$$
\text{Key = Char + Color}
$$

Now, when I'm trying to type a key, I can use its *color* to infer which finger types it.
>[!example] Example

However, some keys are in different rows, that's why i also memorize another piece of info. with each key: 

*how to reach it from the home row*.

$$
\text{Key = Char + Color + Movement}
$$

I'll demonstrate this on the final left-hand visual.
### Home Row keys
